{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 38.74 0 L 68.12 -27.67}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}{{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=V^{12}_{\mu\nu}\frac{T^{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}}{s}V^{34}_{\sigma\rho}.\end{split}

(195)

After some algebra making use of the massless external kinematics, we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{\mu\nu}^{12}(y,\alpha)T^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}V_{\rho\sigma}^{34}(y,\beta)&=\frac{\kappa^{2}}{16}\Big(\alpha^{\mu}\alpha^{\nu}\mathcal{T}_{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}\beta^{\sigma}\beta^{\rho}-\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}\Big)\\
&=\frac{\kappa^{2}}{16}\alpha^{\mu}\alpha^{\nu}\big(\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\sigma}+\eta_{\mu\sigma}\eta_{\nu\rho}-2\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\rho\sigma}\big)\beta^{\rho}\beta^{\sigma}.\end{split}

(196)

Let’s now compare (
196
) with the square of the s-channel scalar QCD numerator

\displaystyle\begin{split}n_{s}^{2}=\alpha^{\mu}\alpha^{\nu}\big(\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\sigma}+\eta_{\mu\sigma}\eta_{\nu\rho}\big)\beta^{\rho}\beta^{\sigma}.\end{split}

(197)

Clearly, the third term in (
196
) is not generated by this procedure. The reason is that when we square a gluon, this gives a 2-form and dilaton in addition to a graviton and the third term in (
196
) is cancelled by the dilaton exchange arising from the double copy. Hence, we must subtract the dilaton contribution in order to obtain a graviton propagator in each channel. On the other hand, this contribution vanishes after summing over all three channels since
\alpha_{\mu}\alpha^{\mu}=\beta_{\mu}\beta^{\mu}=-s
, so after dividing the third term in (
196
) by
s
and summing over all three channels we get
s+t+u=0
. Hence, we still get a graviton exchange amplitude even if we do not explicitly remove the dilaton contribution after taking the double copy of a gluon amplitude. In contrast, this contribution does not cancel out after summing over channels in dS
4
so we must explicitly subtract it when implementing the double copy of scalar QCD correlators, as described in section
4.5
.

Appendix E
Relation to non-local formulations

In this Appendix we relate our local shadow formulations of photons and gravitons coupled to scalars to non-local formulations which are more commonly used in the literature
72
;
71
;
86
;
87
;
18
;
73
;
19
;
75
. We note that local formulations are in-principle more convenient for computing higher-point correlation functions (see
63
for a discussion of computing higher-point vertices using a non-local action).

QED

First we derive a non-local shadow action for scalar QED by using the Gauss law to solve for the longitudinal component of the gauge field. This is equivalent to integrating out the longitudinal component of the field from the path integral. By restricting to the linear sector in the action (
39
) we get
68

\displaystyle\begin{split}S=-\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{d\eta d^{3}x}{\eta^{4}}A_{Ti}(\partial^{2})A_{T}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{d\eta d^{3}x}{\eta^{4}}J^{0}(z,\vec{x})\frac{1}{\partial^{2}}J^{0}(\eta,\vec{x})\end{split}

(198)

The first term denotes the transverse part of the gauge field and the second term arises by integrating out the longitudinal mode and is a contact term with
J^{(0)}(\eta,\vec{x})
given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}J^{0}(\eta,\vec{x})&=\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{\eta}\phi-\phi\partial_{\eta}\phi^{\dagger}.\end{split}

(199)

For scalar QED the current is quadratic in the field and hence this a 4-point interaction term. This term contains an inverse Laplacian and hence is non-local.

From this we can easily obtain a shadow action by performing the procedure described in section
3.2
resulting in the following non-local action:

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{\rm non-local}^{\rm shadow}&=-\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{dzd^{3}x}{z^{4}}\Big[A_{Ti}^{-}(\partial^{2})A_{i}^{-T}-A_{Ti}^{+}(\partial^{2})A_{i}^{+T}\Big]\\
&\qquad+ig\eta^{\mu\nu}\bigg[A_{\mu}^{+}\big({\phi^{-}}^{\dagger}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{+}-\phi^{+}\partial_{\nu}{\phi^{-}}^{\dagger}+{\phi^{+}}^{\dagger}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{-}-\phi^{-}\partial_{\nu}{\phi^{+}}^{\dagger}\big)\\
&+A_{\mu}^{-}\big({\phi^{+}}^{\dagger}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{+}-\phi^{+}\partial_{\nu}{\phi^{+}}^{\dagger}-({\phi^{-}}^{\dagger}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{-}-\phi^{-}\partial_{\nu}{\phi^{-}}^{\dagger})\big)\bigg]\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{dzd^{3}x}{z^{4}}\Big[\big(J_{-+}^{0}(z,\vec{x})+J_{+-}^{0}(z,\vec{x})\big)\frac{1}{\partial^{2}}\big(J_{-+}^{0}(z,\vec{x})+J_{+-}^{0}(z,\vec{x})\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad-\big(J_{--}^{0}(z,\vec{x})+J_{++}^{0}(z,\vec{x})\big)\frac{1}{\partial^{2}}\big(J_{--}^{0}(z,\vec{x})+J_{++}^{0}(z,\vec{x})\big)\Big],\end{split}

(200)

where we use the notation

\displaystyle\begin{split}J^{0}_{ab}=\phi^{a\dagger}\partial_{z}\phi^{b}-\phi^{b}\partial_{z}\phi^{a\dagger}\end{split}

(201)

with
a,b=\pm
. The same action is obtained if one integrates out the longitudinal degrees of freedom from the full shadow action (
39
).

Next let us compare the 4-point photon exchange correlator with external scalars obtained using the non-local action in (
200
) to what we obtain using the local action in section
3
. Note that we only consider
-
fields on the external legs, as before.
Using the action (
200
), the s-channel contribution to the correlator is given by a sum of two terms:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi^{\dagger}_{2}\phi_{3}\phi^{\dagger}_{4}}_{(s)}=C_{T}+C_{L}\end{split}

(202)

where
C_{T}
and
C_{L}
denote the transverse and longitudinal parts of the photon exchange, respectively. The transverse part is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{T}&=\alpha^{i}\beta^{j}\pi_{ij}\int_{0}^{\infty}dz_{1}dz_{2}e^{-k_{12}z_{1}}e^{-k_{34}z_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dp\frac{\cos(pz_{1})\cos(pz_{2})}{p^{2}+y^{2}}\\
&=\alpha^{i}\beta^{j}\pi_{ij}\Big(\frac{1}{(k_{12}+k_{34})(k_{12}+y)(k_{34}+y)}+\frac{1}{(k)(k_{12}+y)(k_{34}+y)}\Big),\end{split}

(203)

which is exactly the transverse part of the correlator found in section
3.2
. The longitudinal part is given by a single diagram with four external legs:

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{4}^{L}=\int_{0}^{\infty}dzj_{--}^{z}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{k}_{2},z)\frac{1}{y^{2}}j_{--}^{z}(\vec{k}_{3},\vec{k}_{4},,z),\end{split}

(204)

where the function
j_{--}^{z}(z)
is obtained from (
201
) by plugging in the form of the bulk-boundary propagators and is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}j^{z}_{--}(\vec{k}_{a},\vec{k}_{b},z)=(k_{a}-k_{b})e^{-k_{ab}z}.\end{split}

(205)

By performing the integral in
z
in (
204
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{4}^{L}=\frac{(k_{1}-k_{2})(k_{3}-k_{4})}{k_{12}+k_{34}},\end{split}

(206)

we find an explicit match with the longitudinal part of the answer computed using the local form of the action given in (
53
).

However it is also possible to show more formally how the form of the answer found using the local form matches with the answer from the non-local form using integration by parts (IBP). To do this we start with the expression for the longitudinal part obtained using the local form of the action as given in section
3.2
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{4,local}^{L}&=\int_{zx}J^{i}(\vec{x},z)G_{ij}^{L}(\vec{x},z;\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})J^{i}(\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})\end{split}

(207)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{L}_{ij}(\vec{x},z;\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})&=\int d^{3}ke^{i\vec{k}\cdot(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp\sin(pz)\sin(pz^{\prime})}{p^{2}}\frac{y_{i}y_{j}}{y^{2}}.\end{split}

(208)

The exact form of
J^{i}
will not be relevant here, but we will only be using the conservation equation

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{i}J^{i}=-\partial_{z}J^{z},\end{split}

(209)

where
\partial_{i}\equiv\partial_{x_{i}}
. We now use the following identity:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int d^{3}ye^{i\vec{y}\cdot(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp\sin(pz)\sin(pz^{\prime})}{p^{2}}\frac{y_{i}y_{j}}{y^{2}}=-\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x^{\prime}_{j}}\int d^{3}ye^{i\vec{y}\cdot(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp\sin(pz)\sin(pz^{\prime})}{p^{2}y^{2}},\end{split}

(210)

and also use the shorthand notation
\int_{xz}
to denote

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{xz}=\int d^{3}xd^{3}x^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}dzdz^{\prime}\end{split}

Thus
C_{4,local}^{L}
becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{4,local}^{L}&=-\int_{zx}J^{i}(\vec{x},z)J^{i}(\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x^{\prime}_{j}}\int d^{3}ye^{i\vec{y}\cdot(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp\sin(pz)\sin(pz^{\prime})}{p^{2}y^{2}}.\end{split}

(211)

After performing IBP and using (
209
) we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{4,local}^{L}=-\int_{zx}\partial_{z}J^{z}(\vec{x},z)\partial_{z^{\prime}}J^{z}(\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})\int d^{3}ye^{i\vec{y}\cdot(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp\sin(pz)\sin(pz^{\prime})}{p^{2}y^{2}}.\end{split}

(212)

Moreover repeated use of IBP gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{4,local}^{L}&=\int_{zx}J^{z}(\vec{x},z)\frac{1}{\partial^{\prime 2}}J^{z}(\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})\delta(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})\delta(z-z^{\prime})\\
&=\int d^{3}xdzJ^{z}(\vec{x},z)\frac{1}{\partial^{2}}J^{z}(\vec{x},z),\end{split}

(213)

recovering the non-local form of the answer (
204
).

Gravity

Let us now sketch how to match graviton exchange correlators with external scalars obtained using local and non-local formulations. A key step is to use stress-tensor conservation equation. The stress tensor of the massless scalar is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial_{\nu}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}(\partial\phi)^{2}\end{split}

(214)

and this satisfies the conservation equations

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{j}(\sqrt{g}T^{ji})&=-z^{2}\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}T^{zi}}{z^{2}}\Big)\\
\sqrt{g}\delta_{ij}T^{ij}&=z^{2}\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}}{z}T^{zz}\Big)-z\partial_{i}\big(\sqrt{g}T^{iz}\big)~.\end{split}

(215)

Moreover, let us recall that the longitudinal part of the wavefunction coefficient
\psi_{4}
is the only contribution to the longitudinal part of the correlator, since the polarisation sum in the
\frac{\psi_{3}\psi_{3}}{\psi_{2}}
term makes it transverse and traceless (
178
). We therefore split
\psi_{4}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{4}=\psi_{4}^{TT}+\psi_{4}^{TL}+\psi_{4}^{LL}\end{split}

(216)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{4}^{TL}&=\int_{zx}T^{ij}(\vec{x},z)G_{ijab}^{TL}(\vec{x},z;\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})T^{ab}(\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime}),\\
\psi_{4}^{LL}&=\int_{zx}T^{ij}(\vec{x},z)G_{ijab}^{LL}(\vec{x},z;\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})T^{ab}(\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime}),\end{split}

(217)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}G_{ijab}^{TL}(\vec{x},z;\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})&=\int d^{3}ye^{i\vec{y}\cdot(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})}(zz^{\prime})^{-3/2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dpJ_{3/2}(pz)J_{3/2}(pz^{\prime})\frac{y_{i}y_{j}\pi_{ab}+\text{perms.}}{y^{2}}\frac{1}{p}\\
G_{ijab}^{LL}(\vec{x},z;\vec{x}^{\prime},z^{\prime})&=\int d^{3}ye^{i\vec{y}\cdot(\vec{x}-\vec{x}^{\prime})}(zz^{\prime})^{-1/2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dpJ_{3/2}(pz)J_{3/2}(pz^{\prime})\frac{y_{i}y_{j}y_{a}y_{b}}{y^{4}}\frac{p^{2}+y^{2}}{p^{3}}.\end{split}

(218)

We note that
\psi_{4}^{TT}
can be mapped to the transverse-traceless part of the in-in correlator by flipping the sign on the spectral parameter in the Bessel functions of the bulk-to-bulk propagators, so we focus our attention on the remaining terms. Using IBP and (
215
), after a tedious calculation we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{4}^{TL}+\psi_{4}^{LL}=R_{1}+R_{2}+R_{3},\end{split}

(219)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}R_{1}&=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dz}{z^{2}}T_{zb}(z)\frac{1}{\partial^{2}}T_{zb}(z),\\
R_{2}&=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dz}{z}\partial_{a}T_{za}(z)\frac{1}{\partial^{2}}T_{zz}(z),\\
R_{3}&=\int_{0}^{\infty}dz\frac{1}{z^{2}}\partial_{a}T_{za}(z)\frac{1}{\partial^{4}}\partial_{b}T_{zb}(z).\end{split}

(220)

From the local form of the action, we found that the s-channel contribution to the graviton exchange correlator is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}=M_{4}^{TT}+\tilde{M}_{4}\end{split}

(221)

where
M_{4}^{TT}
is given by (
111
) and
\tilde{M}_{4}
is given by (
113
). The first term in (
221
),
M_{4}^{TT}
, encodes the transverse-traceless part of the graviton exchange and matches
\widehat{W}+\widehat{G}
in equations 4.25 and 5.5-5.8 of
48
. This shows how the shadow formalism naturally encodes the transverse-traceless piece into a single term. The second term in (
221
),
\tilde{M}_{4}
, matches with the
R
terms discussed above, which are given in equations 4.27 - 4.30 of
48
. See the relevant Mathematica file in
49
for more details.

Appendix F
Relation to in-out formalism

In
36
, certain dressing rules were derived for uplifting scattering amplitudes in flat space to in-in correlators for a variety of scalar theories in dS
4
and in this paper we extend this story to photons, gluons, and gravitons coupled to scalar fields. It has also been pointed out that certain in-in correlators be computed using an in-out formalism
43
. In this Appendix we point out how one can derive the dressing rules from the in-out formalism in certain cases.

Review the In-Out formalism

We provide a lightning review of the propagators in the in-out formalism in flat space and we refer the reader to
64
;
43
for details. The in-in correlator is defined as,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi(\vec{k}_{1})\cdots\phi(\vec{k}_{n})}=\braket{\Psi|\phi(\vec{k}_{1})\cdots\phi(\vec{k}_{n})|\Psi}\end{split}

(222)

where
\vec{k}
are spatial momenta, the state
\ket{\Psi}
is the vacuum state and is obtained via
U(0,-\infty)\ket{0_{F}}
where
\ket{0_{F}}
is the Fock vacuum state and
U=\mathcal{T}e^{-i\int_{-\infty}^{t}H_{int}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}}
. The fields
\phi(\vec{k})
are inserted at
t=0
. The basic idea behind the in-out formalism is that there exists a Fock vacuum at
t\to\infty
through which one can back-evolve in time to obtain the ket
\ket{\Psi}
. This allows one to rewrite (
222
) as a single time path integral,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\Psi|\phi(\vec{k}_{1})\cdots\phi(\vec{k}_{n})|\Psi}&=\braket{0_{F}|\mathcal{T}\Bigl\{U(-\infty,\infty)\phi(\vec{k}_{1})\cdots\phi(\vec{k}_{n})\Bigr\}|0_{F}}\end{split}

(223)

in the absence of subtleties at the turning point in the integral at
t=0
. Hence the propagators appearing in the computation are same as the ones for the un-amputated scattering amplitude. The bulk-bulk propagator is given as the (time-ordered) Feynman propagator,

\displaystyle\begin{split}G(\eta_{1},\eta_{2},k)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{\omega^{2}-k^{2}+i\epsilon}e^{-i\omega(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2})}\end{split}

(224)

where
k=\vec{k}
. The bulk-boundary propagators can be obtained as a limit of the bulk-bulk propagator

\displaystyle\begin{split}B(\eta,k)=\lim_{\eta^{\prime}\to 0}G(\eta,\eta^{\prime},k)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{\omega^{2}-k^{2}+i\epsilon}e^{-i\omega\eta}=e^{-ik\eta}.\end{split}

(225)

While the bulk-boundary propagator can be written via plane waves (as shown in the final equality of (
225
)), we shall find it convenient to use the expression in the intermediate step.

Relation to dressing rules

We begin by considering a 4-point function in massless
\phi^{3}
theory in flat space for illustration. From the rules of the propagator given above, the
s-
channel contribution to the four point function
\braket{\psi|\phi(\vec{k}_{1})\phi(\vec{k}_{2})\phi(\vec{k}_{3})\phi(\vec{k}_{4})|\psi}
is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}=\prod_{i=1}^{4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{i}}{\omega_{i}^{2}-k_{i}^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{\omega^{2}-y^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\eta_{1}d\eta_{2}e^{-i(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})\eta_{1}}e^{-i(\omega_{3}+\omega_{4})\eta_{2}}e^{-i\omega(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2})}\end{split}

(226)

where we have suppressed
i\epsilon
for simplicity. Performing the time-integrals gives the momentum conserving delta function,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}&=\prod_{i=1}^{4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{i}}{\omega_{i}^{2}-k_{i}^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{\omega^{2}-y^{2}}\delta(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}+\omega)\delta(\omega_{3}+\omega_{4}-\omega)\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{4}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{i}}{\omega_{i}^{2}-k_{i}^{2}}\frac{1}{(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})^{2}-y^{2}}\delta(\omega_{3}+\omega_{4}-\omega_{1}-\omega_{2})\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{3}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{i}}{\omega_{i}^{2}-k_{i}^{2}}\frac{1}{(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}-\omega_{3})^{2}-k_{4}^{2}}\frac{1}{(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})^{2}-y^{2}}\end{split}

(227)

We have suppressed the
i\epsilon
for brevity. By performing the integrals we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}&=\frac{1}{(k_{12}+k_{34})(k_{12}+k)(k_{34}+k)}+\frac{1}{k(k_{12}+k)(k_{34}+k)}\end{split}

(228)

While in this simple example the integrals could be performed by brute force, it will be instructive to perform a variable transform in the intermediate step to obtain an alternate formula for general diagrams.

From the example above it is clear that for any off-shell Feynman diagram at 4-points the time integrals will result in energy conservation:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}&=\prod_{n=1}^{4}\int\frac{d\omega_{n}}{\omega_{n}^{2}+k_{n}^{2}}f(\omega_{L})\delta(\omega_{L}+\omega_{R}),\end{split}

(229)

where
\omega_{L}=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}
,
\omega_{R}=\omega_{3}+\omega_{4}
, and
f(\omega_{L})
is a rational function. We have Wick rotated the energies in this step to make contact with the formulas in previous section. Such off-shell diagrams have been studied in the literature, eg,
29
;
83
.
A few comments are in order:

1.

The function
f(\omega)
depends on the energies only via
\omega_{L}
. This is because in general
f(x)
arises from a product of bulk-bulk propagators (of the form (
224
)) and they are always going to be a function of the four momentum
(\omega,\vec{k})
. For an off-shell Feynman diagram there is no relation between
\omega
and
\vec{k}
. However, since the integrals over time (which are exponential functions) are running from
(-\infty,+\infty)
there is still going to be four-momentum
(\omega,\vec{k})
conservation. Therefore any general bulk-bulk propagator can always be written as a function of
(\omega_{vertex},\vec{k}_{vertex})
where this denotes the sum of off-shell four momenta entering a vertex. For the case of the single exchange, at the left vertex we have
(\omega_{vertex},\vec{k}_{vertex})=(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2})
.

2.

The 4-point calculation above straightforwardly generalizes to any number of points.

3.

This expression also holds for loop integrands as it is valid for any rational
f(\omega_{L})
.

4.

This is true for all interactions including derivative interactions, gauge fields and gravity in axial gauges, but the cases with a single derivative (w.r.t
\partial_{z}
) in the interaction term and half-integer spins will have to be dealt with separately.

We now use the formula (
229
) to derive the dressing rules of
\phi^{3}
theory in flat space (which can be mapped by a Weyl transformation to dS
4
) first obtained in
36
. Note that this theory can be mapped to massless
\phi^{4}
theory in half of flat space via a Weyl transformation. Consider the explicit form of the correlator given in (
229
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}&=\prod_{n=1}^{4}\int\frac{d\omega_{n}}{\omega_{n}^{2}+k_{n}^{2}}f(\omega_{L})\delta(\omega_{L}+\omega_{R})\\
&=\int\frac{d\omega_{1}}{\omega_{1}^{2}+k_{1}^{2}}\cdots\frac{d\omega_{4}}{\omega_{4}^{2}+k_{4}^{2}}f(\omega_{L})\delta(\omega_{L}+\omega_{R})\end{split}

(230)

where
\omega_{L}=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}
and
\omega_{R}=\omega_{3}+\omega_{4}
. We first analyze the terms dependent on
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}d\omega_{2}}{(\omega_{1}^{2}+k_{1}^{2})(\omega_{2}^{2}+k_{2}^{2})}f(\omega_{L}).\end{split}

(231)

Since the function
f(\omega_{L})=f(\omega_{1}+\omega_{2})
is independent of
\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}
we perform the following change of variables:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega_{L}&=\omega_{1}+\omega_{2},\qquad\bar{\omega}_{L}=\omega_{1}-\omega_{2},\end{split}

(232)

Performing a similar manipulation for
\omega_{3},\omega_{4}
, results in the following expression for (
230
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}\\
&=2^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{L}d\omega_{R}d\bar{\omega}_{L}d\bar{\omega}_{R}\ \delta(\omega_{L}+\omega_{R})}{\big((\omega_{L}+\bar{\omega}_{L})^{2}+4k_{1}^{2}\big)\big((\omega_{L}-\bar{\omega}_{L})^{2}+4k_{2}^{2}\big)\big((\omega_{R}+\bar{\omega}_{R})^{2}+4k_{3}^{2}\big)\big((\omega_{R}-\bar{\omega}_{R})^{2}+4k_{4}^{2}\big)}f(\omega_{L}).\end{split}

(233)

Performing the integrals over
\bar{\omega}_{L},\bar{\omega}_{R}
then gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{16}\frac{1}{k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega_{L}d\omega_{R}\frac{k_{12}k_{34}\delta(\omega_{L}+\omega_{R})}{(\omega_{L}^{2}+k_{12}^{2})(\omega_{R}^{2}+k_{34}^{2})}f(\omega_{L}).\end{split}

(234)

For the tree level single exchange this can be be diagrammatically expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\braket{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{3}\phi_{4}}_{(s)}=\hbox to130.49pt{\vbox to100.23pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 36.78925pt\lower-57.10551pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

(235)

where the dashed propagators denote the kernels in (
234
) including the energy-conserving delta function
\delta(\omega_{L}+\omega_{R})
, thus recovering the dressing rules for conformally coupled
\phi^{4}
theory in
36
. A similar argument allows us to derive the dressing rules for photons and gluons in dS
4
, which can be trivially mapped to half of Minkowski space via a Weyl transformation due to classical conformal invariance. When doing so, one has to take care of the residual gauge invariance at the boundary which can be used to fix Dirichlet boundary conditions for the longitudinal components as discussed in section
3.1
.
